In addition, combining (3.26) and (3.28) we infer for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$i=1,\dotsc ,n$$\end{document}i=1,⋯,n that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |(u_{r_k}(z_i)-u_{r_k}(z)) \cdot \phi (z-z_i)| \wedge 1\rightarrow 0, \ \text {pointwise for }\mathcal {H}^{n-1}\text {-a.e.}~z \in \Pi ^\xi \cap \big ( B_1(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\big ), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|(urk(zi)-urk(z))·ϕ(z-zi)|∧1→0,pointwise forHn-1-a.e.z∈Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0)),whenever \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}k→∞. Therefore, for every sufficiently large k (depending on z)\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&|u_{r_k}(z) \cdot \phi (z) - \sum _{i} c_i(z)u_{r_k}(z_i) \cdot \phi (z-z_i)| \wedge 1 \\&\le \sum _i |c_i(z)| |(u_{r_k}(z)-u_{r_k}(z_i))\cdot \phi (z-z_i)| \wedge 1\\&\le \sum _i |c_i(z)| |\hat{\mu }_{u_r}^{ \phi (z-z_i)}|(B_1(0)) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|urk(z)·ϕ(z)-∑ici(z)urk(zi)·ϕ(z-zi)|∧1≤∑i|ci(z)||(urk(z)-urk(zi))·ϕ(z-zi)|∧1≤∑i|ci(z)||μ^urϕ(z-zi)|(B1(0))By (3.27), the above inequality yields that for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{n-1}$$\end{document}Hn-1-a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in \Pi ^{\xi }\cap \big ( B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\big )$$\end{document}z∈Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0))\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |u_{r_k}(z) \cdot \phi (z) - \sum _{i} c_i(z)u_{r_k}(z_i)\cdot \phi (z-z_i)| \rightarrow 0, \ \text { as }k \rightarrow \infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|urk(z)·ϕ(z)-∑ici(z)urk(zi)·ϕ(z-zi)|→0,ask→∞.Thus, by recalling (3.25), we have for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{n-1}$$\end{document}Hn-1-a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$z \in \Pi ^{\xi } \cap \big (B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}}(0)\big )$$\end{document}z∈Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0))3.29\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} |f_{\nu ,\xi }(z) - \sum _{i} c_i(z)u_{r_k}(z_i) \cdot \phi (z-z_i)| \rightarrow 0, \ \text { as }k \rightarrow \infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}|fν,ξ(z)-∑ici(z)urk(zi)·ϕ(z-zi)|→0,ask→∞.We conclude by showing that (3.29) gives a contradiction.

Consider the finite-dimensional real vector subspace V of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^0\big (\Pi ^{\xi } \cap \big (B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}} (0)\big ); \mathbb {R}^{n}\big )$$\end{document}L0(Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0));Rn) generated by the elements \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{v_{ij}:\Pi ^{\xi } \cap \big (B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}} (0) \big ) \rightarrow \mathbb {R}^{n}: i,j =1,\dotsc ,n\}$$\end{document}{vij:Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0))→Rn:i,j=1,⋯,n}, where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_{ij}(z):= c_i(z) \phi _j (z-z_i)$$\end{document}vij(z):=ci(z)ϕj(z-zi) (\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \phi _j:= \phi \cdot e_j$$\end{document}ϕj:=ϕ·ej). Condition (3.29) combined with Proposition 3.16 (recall that pointwise convergence implies convergence in measure) implies that the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f_{\nu ,\xi }$$\end{document}fν,ξ restricted to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Pi ^{\xi } \cap \big (B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}} (0) \big )$$\end{document}Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0)) belongs to V.

Since the generators \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$v_{ij}$$\end{document}vij are all smooth functions, any linear combination of them still belongs to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$C^\infty (\Pi ^{\xi } \cap \big (B_{1}(0) \setminus B_{\frac{1}{2}} (0)\big ); \mathbb {R}^{n})$$\end{document}C∞(Πξ∩(B1(0)\B12(0));Rn), forcing \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_{\nu ,\xi }=0$$\end{document}aν,ξ=0. This is not possible because we assumed at the beginning \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_{\nu , \xi } \ne 0$$\end{document}aν,ξ≠0 and we reach a contradiction. The proof is thus concluded. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We are now in position to prove Theorem 3.7.

Proof of Theorem 3.7

We proceed by induction. Observe that in dimension one the theorem is trivially true. Now, assume that the theorem holds true in dimension \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n-1$$\end{document}n-1. Therefore, from Proposition 3.15 we infer that the measure \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathscr {I}_{u,1}$$\end{document}Iu,1 concentrates on the complement in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω of the set E defined in (3.18). Since by Proposition 3.17 we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \setminus E \subset \{x \in \Omega : \Theta ^{*n-1}(\hat{\mu }_{u,1},x) >0\}$$\end{document}Ω\E⊂{x∈Ω:Θ∗n-1(μ^u,1,x)>0}, then by applying the classical density estimates for Radon measure (cf. [29]) we infer that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \setminus E$$\end{document}Ω\E is \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sigma $$\end{document}σ-finite with respect to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{n-1}$$\end{document}Hn-1. We are thus in position to apply Besicovitch-Federer structure theorem [29] to write \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \setminus E = R \cup U$$\end{document}Ω\E=R∪U where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R \subset \Omega $$\end{document}R⊂Ω is countably \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(n-1)$$\end{document}(n-1)-rectifiable while the Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} Proof of Theorem 3.8

The proof follows the line of [26, Theorem 9.1], we report here only the main differences.

Since (3.37) holds for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x\in \Omega \setminus N_0$$\end{document}x∈Ω\N0 and for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in X_0$$\end{document}ξ∈X0, we deduce that the same is true for (3.36). Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0 \in X_0$$\end{document}ξ0∈X0, we conclude that (3.36) holds for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi =\xi _0$$\end{document}ξ=ξ0 for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in \Omega \setminus N_0$$\end{document}x∈Ω\N0.

By the arbitrariness of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi _0$$\end{document}ξ0 we have shown that for a.e. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}ξ∈Rn we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} e^\xi (x)=e(u)(x)\xi \cdot \xi \quad \text {a.e. in } \Omega . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}eξ(x)=e(u)(x)ξ·ξa.e. inΩ.Lastly, (3.6) follows by combining the above equality and (3.33).

Finally, the fact that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$e(u) \in L^1(\Omega ;\mathbb {M}^{n\times n}_{sym})$$\end{document}e(u)∈L1(Ω;Msymn×n) follows from\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \int _{\Omega }|e(u)(x)| \,\text {d}x&\le c(n) \int _{\Omega }\int _{\mathbb {S}^{n-1}} |e(u)(x)\xi \cdot \xi | \,\text {d}\xi \, \text {d}x\\&= \int _{\mathbb {S}^{n-1}} \int _{\Pi ^\xi } \int _{\Omega _y^\xi } |e(u)_y^\xi (t)\xi \cdot \xi )| \,\text {d}t \,\text {d}y \, \text {d}\xi \\&\le \hat{\mu }_{u,1}(\Omega ) <\infty . \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ω|e(u)(x)|dx≤c(n)∫Ω∫Sn-1|e(u)(x)ξ·ξ|dξdx=∫Sn-1∫Πξ∫Ωyξ|e(u)yξ(t)ξ·ξ)|dtdydξ≤μ^u,1(Ω)<∞.

Proof of Theorem 1.1

We divide this section into two subsections. The first one contains a number of preliminary results that will be exploited in the second subsection, where the proof of Theorem 1.1 is carried out.

Preliminary results

We start with a general estimate.

Lemma 4.1

For every positive integer n it holds true4.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \sup _{e \in \mathbb {R}^{n}} \int _{B_4(0)\setminus B_{1/4}(0)} \frac{|e|}{1+|e \cdot \eta |^2} \, \text {d}\mathcal {H}^n(\eta ) < \infty . \end{aligned}$$\end{document}supe∈Rn∫B4(0)\B1/4(0)|e|1+|e·η|2dHn(η)<∞.

We now recall some useful results shown in [31] for the study of nonlocal approximations of the Mumford-Shah functional. The proofs of Lemmas 4.2-4.4 coincide with those of [31].

a and b are Lebesgue points of u.

if \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in [a+\frac{z}{k},a+\frac{z+1}{k}],$$\end{document}x∈[a+zk,a+z+1k], then \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k(x)$$\end{document}uk(x) belongs to the interval with endpoints \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u(a+\frac{z}{k})$$\end{document}u(a+zk) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u(a+\frac{z+1}{k})$$\end{document}u(a+z+1k);

Lemma 4.4

([31, Lemma 5.1]) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn be an open set. Lemma 4.5

([31, Lemma 5.3]) Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Rn be an open set, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E \Subset \Omega $$\end{document}E⋐Ω, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R \subset \mathbb {R}^{n}$$\end{document}R⊂Rn, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\eta >0$$\end{document}η>0 be such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$R\subset \frac{\Omega - \Omega }{\eta }$$\end{document}R⊂Ω-Ωη and\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \textrm{dist} ( E, \partial \Omega ) \ge \eta \, \sup _{\xi \in R} |\xi |\,. $$\end{document}dist(E,∂Ω)≥ηsupξ∈R|ξ|.Then, for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in L^{0}(\Omega ;\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}u∈L0(Ω;Rn), \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0, and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m \in \mathbb {N}$$\end{document}m∈N such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m \varepsilon <\eta $$\end{document}mε<η we have that4.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{R} F_{ m \varepsilon , \xi }(u,E ) \, \textrm{d}\xi \le \int _{R} F_{\varepsilon , \xi }(u, \Omega ) \, \textrm{d}\xi \,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫RFmε,ξ(u,E)dξ≤∫RFε,ξ(u,Ω)dξ.

Proof

Given \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0, we prove the statement by induction over m. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$m=1$$\end{document}m=1 there is nothing to prove. Let us assume that (4.3) holds for m and prove it for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ m+1 $$\end{document}m+1, assuming that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ (m + 1) \varepsilon < \eta $$\end{document}(m+1)ε<η. In particular, this implies that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E \subset \Omega - (m+1) \varepsilon \xi $$\end{document}E⊂Ω-(m+1)εξ for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in R$$\end{document}ξ∈R. For \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A, B \in \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}A,B∈R it holds\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \arctan \bigg ( \frac{(A+ B)^{2}}{ m+1 } \bigg ) \le \arctan (A^{2}) + \arctan \bigg ( \frac{B^{2}}{ m } \bigg ) \,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}arctan((A+B)2m+1)≤arctan(A2)+arctan(B2m).Applying such inequality to\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} A = \frac{ (u(x + ( m+1 ) \varepsilon \xi ) - u(x + m \varepsilon \xi )) \cdot \xi }{ \sqrt{\varepsilon }} \qquad B = \frac{ (u(x + m \varepsilon \xi ) - u(x)) \cdot \xi }{\sqrt{\varepsilon }}\,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}A=(u(x+(m+1)εξ)-u(x+mεξ))·ξεB=(u(x+mεξ)-u(x))·ξε,with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in E$$\end{document}x∈E and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in R$$\end{document}ξ∈R, we get that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\frac{1}{( m+1 )\varepsilon } \arctan \bigg ( \frac{ \big ( (u(x + (m+1) \varepsilon \xi ) - u(x) ) \cdot \xi \big ) ^{2}}{(m +1) \varepsilon } \bigg ) \\&\quad \le \frac{1}{( m +1) \varepsilon } \arctan \bigg ( \frac{ \big ( ( u ( x + (m+1) \varepsilon \xi ) - u( x + m \varepsilon \xi ) ) \cdot \xi \big )^{2}}{\varepsilon } \bigg ) \\&\qquad + \frac{m}{(m+1)}\frac{1}{ m \varepsilon } \arctan \bigg ( \frac{ \big ( (u(x + m \varepsilon \xi ) - u(x)) \cdot \xi \big )^{2}}{ m \varepsilon } \bigg ) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1(m+1)εarctan(((u(x+(m+1)εξ)-u(x))·ξ)2(m+1)ε)≤1(m+1)εarctan(((u(x+(m+1)εξ)-u(x+mεξ))·ξ)2ε)+m(m+1)1mεarctan(((u(x+mεξ)-u(x))·ξ)2mε)Integrating over \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \in E$$\end{document}x∈E and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\xi \in R$$\end{document}ξ∈R and performing a change of variable in the first integral on the right-hand side we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{R} F_{( m+1 )\varepsilon , \xi } (u, E) \, \textrm{d}\xi&\le \frac{1}{( m+1 ) \varepsilon } \int _{R} \int _{E + m \varepsilon \xi } \arctan \bigg ( \frac{ \big ( ( u ( x + \varepsilon \xi ) - u( x ) ) \cdot \xi \big )^{2}}{\varepsilon } \bigg ) \, \textrm{d}x \, \textrm{d}\xi \\&\qquad + \frac{m}{(m+1)} \int _{R} F_{ m \varepsilon , \xi } (u, E) \, \textrm{d}\xi \\&\le \frac{1}{( m +1)} \int _{R} F_{\varepsilon , \xi } (u, \Omega ) \, \textrm{d}\xi + \frac{m}{(m+1)} \int _{R} F_{ m \varepsilon , \xi } (u, E) \, \textrm{d}\xi \,, \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫RF(m+1)ε,ξ(u,E)dξ≤1(m+1)ε∫R∫E+mεξarctan(((u(x+εξ)-u(x))·ξ)2ε)dxdξ+m(m+1)∫RFmε,ξ(u,E)dξ≤1(m+1)∫RFε,ξ(u,Ω)dξ+m(m+1)∫RFmε,ξ(u,E)dξ,where, in the last inequality, we have used that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$E + m \varepsilon \xi \subset \Omega \cap \Omega - \varepsilon \xi $$\end{document}E+mεξ⊂Ω∩Ω-εξ. Hence, we conclude for (4.3) by the induction hypothesis. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We conclude this section with the following lemma, which characterizes the density \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi _{p}$$\end{document}φp and the constant \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\beta _p$$\end{document}βp appearing in (1.7) and in the definition of the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p}$$\end{document}Fp in (1.10).

\end{aligned}$$\end{document}μ(u,A):=supBA∑B∈BAζ(u,B),where \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} Then, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ can be extended to a Borel measure (which we still denote by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ with a slight abuse of notation). We also observe that there exists a positive constant C only depending on n and p such that for every open set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \subset \Omega $$\end{document}A⊂Ω we have4.4\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mu (u,A) \le C \mathcal {G}_{\frac{2}{\pi }}(u,A), \end{aligned}$$\end{document}μ(u,A)≤CG2π(u,A),where the inequality follows by the definition of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ. In particular, (4.4) holds for every Borel set. Hence, we decompose \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mu $$\end{document}μ in the following way\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mu (u,B)= \int _{B} f(x)\, \text {d}x + \int _{J_u \cap B} g(x) \, \text {d}\mathcal {H}^{n-1}(x) \end{aligned}$$\end{document}μ(u,B)=∫Bf(x)dx+∫Ju∩Bg(x)dHn-1(x)for every Borel set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B \subset \Omega $$\end{document}B⊂Ω, for some densities f and g.

Remark 4.7

We observe that, in the case \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$p=1$$\end{document}p=1, the function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varphi _1$$\end{document}φ1 admits a more explicit representation: for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \in \mathbb {M}^{n\times n}_{sym}$$\end{document}A∈Msymn×n we have4.19\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \varphi _1(A)=\int _{\mathbb {R}^n} \langle A \xi , \xi \rangle ^2 e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi = \frac{\pi ^{n/2}}{4} (2|A|^2+\text {tr}(A)^2). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}φ1(A)=∫Rn⟨Aξ,ξ⟩2e-|ξ|2dξ=πn/24(2|A|2+tr(A)2).To show it we proceed by induction on the dimension n. We first observe that, without loss of generality, we can assume the matrix A to be diagonal, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$a_i:=A_{ii}$$\end{document}ai:=Aii. If \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n=1$$\end{document}n=1, by a simple calculation we get\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \int _{\mathbb {R}}a^2 \xi ^4 e^{-\xi ^2} \text {d}\xi = \frac{\pi ^{1/2}}{4} 3a^2. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}∫Ra2ξ4e-ξ2dξ=π1/243a2.Assume now that the thesis holds up to dimension \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$n-1,$$\end{document}n-1, then we obtain\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\int _{\mathbb {R}^n} \left( \Big ( \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} a_i \xi _i^2 \Big )^2 + a_n^2 \xi _n^4 + 2 \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} a_i \xi _i^2 a_n \xi _n^2 \right) e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi \\&= \frac{\pi ^{n/2}}{4} \big (2 |A|^2_{n-1}+\text {tr}_{n-1}(A)^2\big ) + \frac{\pi ^{n/2}}{4} (2a_n^2+a_n^2) + \frac{\pi ^{n/2}}{4} \sum _{i=1}^{n-1} 2 a_i a_n \\&= \frac{\pi ^{n/2}}{4} \big (2|A|^2+\text {tr}(A)^2\big ), Proof of Theorem 1.1

Consider any subsequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon _k \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}εk→0 and set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_k:=u_{\varepsilon _k}$$\end{document}uk:=uεk. Using a similar argument as above, we prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^0(J_{\hat{u}_{y,\lambda }^{\xi }} \cap I)$$\end{document}H0(Ju^y,λξ∩I) is greater than or equal to the number of connected components of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_y^\xi \cap I$$\end{document}Ayξ∩I and is uniformly bounded, from which it follows that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A_y^\xi $$\end{document}Ayξ is the union of a finite number of intervals. Finally, (4.49) is a consequence of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^0(\partial ^*A_y^\xi \cap I)\le \mathcal {H}^0(J_{\hat{u}_{y,\lambda }^\xi } \cap I)$$\end{document}H0(∂∗Ayξ∩I)≤H0(Ju^y,λξ∩I) and of (4.45).

To conclude we need to prove that A is of finite perimeter. Multiplying (4.49) by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C(n) \mathcal {H}^{n-1}(\partial ^* A \cap B)&\le \int _{\mathbb {R}^n} \Big ( \int _{\Pi ^\xi } \mathcal {H}^0(\partial ^* A_y^\xi infk→∞Fk1(uk,B),where the last inequality follows from Fatou’s Lemma. This proves in particular that A has locally finite perimeter in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω. By Besicovitch covering Theorem there exist a dimensional constant j(n) and \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {B}_1, \ldots , \mathcal {B}_{j(n)}$$\end{document}B1,…,Bj(n) countable families of pairwise disjoint open balls contained in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω, such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \cup _{i=1}^{j(n)} \cup _{B \in \mathcal {B}_i} B$$\end{document}Ω⊂∪i=1j(n)∪B∈BiB. Hence\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} C(n)\mathcal {H}^{n-1}(\partial ^* A)&\le \sum _{i=1}^{j(n)} \sum _{B \in \mathcal {B}_i} C(n) \mathcal {H}^{n-1}(\partial ^* A \cap B) \\&\le \sum _{i=1}^{j(n)} \sum _{B \in \mathcal {B}_i}\liminf _{k \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {F}^1_{\varepsilon _k}(u_k,B) \\&\le j(n) \liminf _{k \rightarrow \infty } \sum _{B \in \mathcal {B}_i}\mathcal {F}^1_{\varepsilon _k}(u_k,B) \\&\le j(n) \liminf _{k \rightarrow \infty }\mathcal {F}^1_{\varepsilon _k}(u_k,\Omega ) <\infty , \end{aligned}$$\end{document}C(n)Hn-1(∂∗A)≤∑i=1j(n)∑B∈BiC(n)Hn-1(∂∗A∩B)≤∑i=1j(n)∑B∈Bilim infk→∞Fk1(uk,B)≤j(n)lim infk→∞∑B∈BiFk1(uk,B)≤j(n)lim infk→∞Fk1(uk,Ω)<∞,where, in the last but one inequality above, we used Remark 2.12.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence and convergence of quasi-minimisers

This section is devoted to the proof of Theorem 1.2 as well as to the convergence, up to subsequences, of quasi-minimizers of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p}_{\varepsilon }$$\end{document}Fεp under Dirichlet boundary conditions. The former is performed in Section 5.1, the latter in Section 5.2.

\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence

We start with the following pointwise convergence result following the strategy in [31, Theorem 3.4].

Proposition 5.1

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I \subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}I⊂R be an interval and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \text {GSBV}(I)$$\end{document}u∈GSBV(I). For every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0, it holds5.1\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} F_{\varepsilon }(u,I \cap (I-\varepsilon ))\le \frac{\pi }{2}\mathcal{M}\mathcal{S}_{\frac{2}{\pi }}(u,I). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Fε(u,I∩(I-ε))≤π2MS2π(u,I).

Proof

Since I is an interval, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$I \cap (I - \varepsilon )$$\end{document}I∩(I-ε) is also an interval or it is empty. Assume that it is not empty otherwise there is nothing to prove. Define \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A^\varepsilon := \{t \in I \cap (I -\varepsilon ): [t,t+\varepsilon ] \cap J_u \ne \emptyset \}$$\end{document}Aε:={t∈I∩(I-ε):[t,t+ε]∩Ju≠∅}. Notice that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$t \in I \cap (I -\varepsilon )$$\end{document}t∈I∩(I-ε) implies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$[t,t+\varepsilon ] \subset I$$\end{document}[t,t+ε]⊂I. We have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \frac{1}{\varepsilon }\int _{A_\varepsilon } \arctan \bigg (\frac{(u(t+\varepsilon )- u(t))^2}{\varepsilon } \bigg ) \text {d}t \le \frac{\pi }{2 \varepsilon } |A_\varepsilon | \le \frac{\pi }{2} \mathcal {H}^0(J_u)\,. $$\end{document}1ε∫Aεarctan((u(t+ε)-u(t))2ε)dt≤π2ε|Aε|≤π2H0(Ju).Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\arctan x \le x$$\end{document}arctanx≤x for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$x \ge 0$$\end{document}x≥0, we have\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned}&\frac{1}{\varepsilon }\int _{(I \cap (I-\varepsilon ))\setminus A_\varepsilon } \arctan \bigg (\frac{(u(t+\varepsilon )- u(t))^2}{\varepsilon } \bigg ) \text {d}t \\&\le \frac{1}{\varepsilon } \int _{(I \cap (I-\varepsilon ))\setminus A_\varepsilon } \arctan \bigg (\int _t^{t + \varepsilon } |\nabla u(\tau )|^2\, \text {d}\tau \bigg ) \text {d}t\\&\le \frac{1}{\varepsilon } \int _{(I \cap (I-\varepsilon ))\setminus A_\varepsilon } \int _t^{t + \varepsilon } |\nabla u(\tau )|^2\, \text {d}\tau \,\text {d}t\le \int _{I} |\nabla u(t)|^2 \text {d}t. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}1ε∫(I∩(I-ε))\Aεarctan((u(t+ε)-u(t))2ε)dt≤1ε∫(I∩(I-ε))\Aεarctan(∫tt+ε|∇u(τ)|2dτ)dt≤1ε∫(I∩(I-ε))\Aε∫tt+ε|∇u(τ)|2dτdt≤∫I|∇u(t)|2dt.By combining the above two inequalities we conclude the proof. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

We are now in a position to conclude the proof of Theorem 1.2.

Proof of Theorem 1.2

We notice that the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-liminf inequality is a direct consequence of Theorem 1.1 (cf. (1.7)). To conclude for the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence, it is enough to show that for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in L^{0} (\Omega ; \mathbb {R}^{n})$$\end{document}u∈L0(Ω;Rn) it holds true5.2\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \mathcal {F}^{p}_{\varepsilon } (u, \Omega ) \le \mathcal {F}^{p} (u, \Omega )\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limε→0Fεp(u,Ω)≤Fp(u,Ω).We begin by recalling that\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \begin{aligned} \mathcal {F}^p_{\varepsilon }(u,\Omega )&=\sup _{\mathscr {B}}\sum _{B \in \mathscr {B}}\left( \int _{\frac{\Omega -\Omega }{\varepsilon }} F_{\varepsilon ,\xi }(u,B)^p e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi \right) ^{\frac{1}{p}}\\&=\sup _{\mathscr {B}}\sum _{B \in \mathscr {B}}\left( \int _{\frac{\Omega -\Omega }{\varepsilon }} \left( \int _{\Pi ^\xi }F_{\varepsilon }(\hat{u}_y^\xi ,B_y^\xi \cap (B-\varepsilon \xi )_y^\xi ) \text {d}y \right) ^p |\xi |^p e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi \right) ^{\frac{1}{p}}, \end{aligned} \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Fεp(u,Ω)=supB∑B∈B∫Ω-ΩεFε,ξ(u,B)pe-|ξ|2dξ1p=supB∑B∈B∫Ω-Ωε∫ΠξFε(u^yξ,Byξ∩(B-εξ)yξ)dyp|ξ|pe-|ξ|2dξ1p,where the supremum is taken over the set of finite families of disjoint open balls contained in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega $$\end{document}Ω.

Then, since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$(B-\varepsilon \xi )^\xi _y=B^\xi _y -\varepsilon $$\end{document}(B-εξ)yξ=Byξ-ε and since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$B^\xi _y \subset \mathbb {R}$$\end{document}Byξ⊂R is an interval, we combine the above equation with (5.1) and we deduce\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0}\mathcal {F}^p_{\varepsilon }(u,\Omega )&\le \limsup _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \sup _{\mathscr {B}}\sum _{B \in \mathscr {B}} \frac{\pi }{2} \left( \int _{\frac{\Omega -\Omega }{\varepsilon }} \left( \int _{\Pi ^\xi } \mathcal{M}\mathcal{S}_{\frac{2}{\pi }}(\hat{u}_y^\xi ,B_y^\xi ) \text {d}y \right) ^p |\xi |^p e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi \right) ^{\frac{1}{p}}\\&\le \sup _{\mathscr {B}} \sum _{B \in \mathscr {B}} \frac{\pi }{2} \left( \int _{\mathbb {R}^n} \left( \int _{\Pi ^\xi } \mathcal{M}\mathcal{S}_{\frac{2}{\pi }}(\hat{u}_y^\xi , B_y^\xi ) \text {d}y \right) ^p |\xi |^p e^{-|\xi |^2} \text {d}\xi \right) ^{\frac{1}{p}}. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}lim supε→0Fεp(u,Ω)≤lim supε→0supB∑B∈Bπ2∫Ω-Ωε∫ΠξMS2π(u^yξ,Byξ)dyp|ξ|pe-|ξ|2dξ1p≤supB∑B∈Bπ2∫Rn∫ΠξMS2π(u^yξ,Byξ)dyp|ξ|pe-|ξ|2dξ1p.Thanks to Lemma 4.6 we infer (5.2). This concludes the proof of the theorem. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□

Convergence of quasi-minimisers

For the purposes of this subsection, we fix two open sets \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega \subset \Omega ' \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω⊂Ω′⊂Rn. We further assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _D \Omega $$\end{document}∂DΩ, namely, the Dirichlet part of the boundary, satisfies \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _D \Omega = \partial \Omega \cap \Omega '$$\end{document}∂DΩ=∂Ω∩Ω′. As it is customary in free discontinuity problems, we consider a relaxed boundary condition on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _D \Omega $$\end{document}∂DΩ. To this purpose, our Dirichlet datum is given by a function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f :\partial _D \Omega \rightarrow \mathbb {R}^n $$\end{document}f:∂DΩ→Rn which we identify, with a slight abuse of notation, with the trace on \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _{D} \Omega $$\end{document}∂DΩ of a function \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in H^{1}(\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}f∈H1(Ω′;Rn). The domain of our functionals is thus denoted by \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$L^0_f(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}Lf0(Ω′;Rn) and defined as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ L^0_f(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^m):= \big \{u \in L^0(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n) : u= f \text { a.e. in }\Omega '\setminus \Omega \big \}. $$\end{document}Lf0(Ω′;Rm):={u∈L0(Ω′;Rn):u=fa.e. inΩ′\Ω}.In addition we define for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0 the functionals \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p, f}_{\varepsilon }:L^{0} (\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^{n}) \rightarrow [0, \infty ]$$\end{document}Fεp,f:L0(Ω′;Rn)→[0,∞] as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {F}^{p,f}_{\varepsilon }(u,\Omega ') := {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \mathcal {F}^p_{\varepsilon }(u,\Omega ') & \text {if } u \in L^0_f(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n) \\ + \infty & \text {otherwise in } L^0(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n) \end{array}\right. } $$\end{document}Fεp,f(u,Ω′):=Fεp(u,Ω′)ifu∈Lf0(Ω′;Rn)+∞otherwise inL0(Ω′;Rn)and the limit functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p,f} :L^{0} (\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^{n}) \rightarrow [0, \infty ] $$\end{document}Fp,f:L0(Ω′;Rn)→[0,∞] as\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {F}^{p,f}(u,\Omega ') := {\left\{ \begin{array}{ll} \mathcal {F}^p(u,\Omega ') & \text {if } u \in \text {GSBD}(\Omega ') \cap L^0_f(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n) \\ + \infty & \text {otherwise in } L^0(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n). \end{array}\right. } $$\end{document}Fp,f(u,Ω′):=Fp(u,Ω′)ifu∈GSBD(Ω′)∩Lf0(Ω′;Rn)+∞otherwise inL0(Ω′;Rn).We further observe that the functional \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p, f}$$\end{document}Fp,f takes the form\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$ \mathcal {F}^{p, f} (u,\Omega ')= \int _{\Omega '} \varphi _{p} (e (u)) \, \text {d}x + \beta _{p} \bigg (\mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u \cap \Omega ) + \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u \cap \partial _D \Omega )$$\end{document}Hn-1(Ju∩∂DΩ) penalizes the part of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} condition is not attained, namely, the set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u^-(x)$$\end{document}u-(x) denotes the trace with respect to the inner normal to \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial \Omega $$\end{document}∂Ω of u at x.

We have the following theorem.

Theorem 5.2

Let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega , \Omega ' \subset \mathbb {R}^n$$\end{document}Ω,Ω′⊂Rn be two open sets, let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial _D \Omega = \partial \Omega \cap \Omega '$$\end{document}∂DΩ=∂Ω∩Ω′ be the Dirichlet part of the boundary, and let \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$f \in H^{1}(\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^n)$$\end{document}f∈H1(Ω′;Rn) be an admissible Dirichlet datum as above. Assume that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{u_\varepsilon \}_{\varepsilon >0}$$\end{document}{uε}ε>0 is a family of quasi-minimisers for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\{\mathcal {F}^{p,f}_\varepsilon \}_{\varepsilon >0}$$\end{document}{Fεp,f}ε>0, namely,5.3\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \lim _{\varepsilon \rightarrow 0} \bigg ( \mathcal {F}_\varepsilon ^{p,f}(u_\varepsilon ,\Omega ')- \inf _{w \in L^0(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^n)} \mathcal {F}_\varepsilon ^{p,f}(w,\Omega ') \bigg ) = 0\,. \end{aligned}$$\end{document}limε→0(Fεp,f(uε,Ω′)-infw∈L0(Ω′;Rn)Fεp,f(w,Ω′))=0.Then, there exist a minimizer \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \textrm{GSBD} (\Omega ') \cap L^{0}_{f} (\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^{n})$$\end{document}u∈GSBD(Ω′)∩Lf0(Ω′;Rn) of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p, f}$$\end{document}Fp,f and a subsequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon _{k} \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}εk→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}k→∞ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\varepsilon _k}\rightarrow u$$\end{document}uεk→u almost everywhere in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega '$$\end{document}Ω′.

Proof

By (5.2) we have that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{\varepsilon >0}\mathcal {F}^{p,f}_\varepsilon (f,\Omega ') < \infty $$\end{document}supε>0Fεp,f(f,Ω′)<∞. Hence, condition (5.3) yields \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\sup _{\varepsilon >0}\mathcal {F}^{p,f}_\varepsilon (u_\varepsilon ,\Omega ') < \infty $$\end{document}supε>0Fεp,f(uε,Ω′)<∞. We are thus in position to apply Theorem 1.1 to infer the existence of a subsequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon _k \rightarrow 0$$\end{document}εk→0 as \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$k \rightarrow \infty $$\end{document}k→∞ and a finite perimeter set \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \subset \Omega '$$\end{document}A⊂Ω′ such that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A = \{ x \in \Omega ': \, |u_{\varepsilon _{k}}| \rightarrow \infty \text { as }k \rightarrow \infty \}$$\end{document}A={x∈Ω′:|uεk|→∞ask→∞}, \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\varepsilon _k} \rightarrow u$$\end{document}uεk→u pointwise a.e. in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega ' \setminus A$$\end{document}Ω′\A for \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u \in \text {GSBD} (\Omega ')$$\end{document}u∈GSBD(Ω′) with \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u=0$$\end{document}u=0 in A. In addition, (1.7) is fulfilled. Since \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_\varepsilon = f$$\end{document}uε=f a.e. in \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega ' \setminus \Omega $$\end{document}Ω′\Ω for every \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\varepsilon >0$$\end{document}ε>0, we deduce \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$A \subset \Omega $$\end{document}A⊂Ω.

It remains to prove that u is actually a minimiser of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p,f}(\cdot ,\Omega ')$$\end{document}Fp,f(·,Ω′). We observe that we cannot immediately infer that u is a minimiser from the \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Gamma $$\end{document}Γ-convergence given by Theorem 1.2, since the sequence \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$u_{\varepsilon _k}$$\end{document}uεk does not convergence in measure to u in the whole of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\Omega '$$\end{document}Ω′. Nevertheless, the proof can be achieved with the very same argument as in the standard case. Indeed, take \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w \in L^{0} (\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^{n})$$\end{document}w∈L0(Ω′;Rn). Since we want to prove that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p,f}(w,\Omega ') \ge \mathcal {F}^{p,f}(u,\Omega ')$$\end{document}Fp,f(w,Ω′)≥Fp,f(u,Ω′), we assume with no loss of generality that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$w \in \text {GSBD}(\Omega ') \cap L^{0}_{f} (\Omega '; \mathbb {R}^{n})$$\end{document}w∈GSBD(Ω′)∩Lf0(Ω′;Rn). By virtue of (1.7), (5.2), and (5.3), we estimate\documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\begin{aligned} \mathcal {F}^{p,f}(w,\Omega ')&=\lim _{k \rightarrow \infty } \mathcal {F}^{p,f}_{\varepsilon _k}(w,\Omega ') \ge \limsup _{k \rightarrow \infty } \inf _{ v \in L^0(\Omega ';\mathbb {R}^m)} \mathcal {F}_\varepsilon ^{p,f}( v,\Omega ') \\&= \limsup _{k \rightarrow \infty }\mathcal {F}_\varepsilon ^{p,f}(u_{\varepsilon _k},\Omega ') \ge \int _{\Omega '} \varphi _{p} (e(u)) \, \text {d}x + \beta _{p} \mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u \cup \partial ^* A) \\&\ge \int _{\Omega '} \varphi _{p} (e(u)) \, \text {d}x + \beta _{p} \mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u)\\&= \int _{\Omega '} \varphi _{p} ( e(u)) \, \text {d}x + \beta _{p} \bigg (\mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u \cap \Omega ) + \mathcal {H}^{n-1}(J_u \cap \partial _D \Omega )\bigg ) = \mathcal {F}^{p,f}(u,\Omega '). \end{aligned}$$\end{document}Fp,f(w,Ω′)=limk→∞Fkp,f(w,Ω′)≥lim supk→∞infv∈L0(Ω′;Rm)Fεp,f(v,Ω′)=lim supk→∞Fεp,f(uεk,Ω′)≥∫Ω′φp(e(u))dx+βpHn-1(Ju∪∂∗A)≥∫Ω′φp(e(u))dx+βpHn-1(Ju)=∫Ω′φp(e(u))dx+βp(Hn-1(Ju∩Ω)+Hn-1(Ju∩∂DΩ))=Fp,f(u,Ω′).Thanks to the arbitrariness of w we deduce that \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\partial ^{*}A \subset J_{u}$$\end{document}∂∗A⊂Ju and that u is a minimizer of \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\mathcal {F}^{p, f}$$\end{document}Fp,f. \documentclass[12pt]{minimal} \usepackage{amsmath} \usepackage{wasysym} \usepackage{amsfonts} \usepackage{amssymb} \usepackage{amsbsy} \usepackage{mathrsfs} \usepackage{upgreek} \setlength{\oddsidemargin}{-69pt} \begin{document}$$\square $$\end{document}□
